Extensive experiments on Metric-Bench demonstrate that our approach significantly enhances spatial metric understanding, outperforming existing and even larger proprietary models by 43.1\%, while improving downstream embodied performance over a spatial-specialized counterpart by 30.4\% on RoboSpatial overall accuracy and 9.3\% on ERQA, and additionally delivering consistent gains on general benchmarks (15.9\% on V$\star$Bench, 88.9\% on BLINK), indicating that the proposed adaptation does not necessarily compromise general VLM capabilities.

</details>


### [47] [Visual Jev: Accurate and Efficient Decisions from Shared Visual Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25845)
*Guanxu Yu,Yuhang Yao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Visual Jev is a vision-language model that encodes images once and executes multiple independent questions as a batch, achieving 8.9x speedup over serial execution while improving accuracy from 70.6% to 76.1% through answer-supervised post-training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision applications often ask multiple independent forced-choice questions about the same image. Current methods either process questions independently (slow) or recompute prefixes for batched questions (inefficient). The authors aim to improve efficiency through shared batched execution while maintaining accuracy.

Method: 1) Encode image and public context once, 2) Execute isolated question suffixes as a batch, 3) Read candidate probabilities from backbone's language-model head, 4) Apply answer-supervised post-training on the backbone

Result: 1) Accuracy improved from 70.6% to 76.1% (equal-weight macro accuracy), 2) At N=32 questions: 8.9x faster than serial execution, 3.4x faster than already-batched baseline, 3) Higher peak memory required, 4) Matched typed-head control shows no consistent accuracy advantage

Conclusion: Simple design is optimal: adapt backbone for quality, retain existing language-model-head readout, and share execution for efficiency. The approach balances accuracy gains with computational efficiency, with gains concentrated on trained task families.

Abstract: Many vision applications ask several independent, forced-choice questions about the same image. Visual Jev encodes the image and public context once, executes isolated question suffixes as a batch, and reads candidate probabilities from the backbone's language-model head. Across four benchmarks, answer-supervised post-training raises equal-weight macro accuracy from 70.6% to 76.1%, with the gain concentrated on the two task families represented in training. At N=32 questions per image, shared batched execution is 8.9x faster in warm amortized time than independent serial execution and remains 3.4x faster than an already-batched baseline that recomputes the prefix, at the cost of higher peak memory. A matched typed-head control offers no consistent accuracy advantage over the language-model-head readout. The supported design is therefore simple: adapt the backbone for quality, retain the existing readout, and share execution for efficiency.

</details>


### [48] [Less Is More in the Long Tail: Stage-Adaptive Sample Selection for Annotation-Efficient Dense Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25850)
*Xiaofei Du,Lei Zhang,Shuyu Yan,Manning Wang,Zhijian Song*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SASS is a stage-adaptive data-selection framework for pool-based active learning in long-tailed dense prediction tasks (e.g., 3D medical segmentation). It uses self-supervised gradient scoring, category rebalancing, and stage-adaptive acquisition to reduce annotation costs while maintaining performance. It achieves 98.3% of full-dataset performance with only 40% annotation budget.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deep learning performance scales with training data, but dense prediction tasks (pixel/voxel-level annotation) are prohibitively expensive, especially with long-tailed category distributions (many rare anatomical structures). Current active learning methods don't adequately address the long-tail imbalance and evolving model representations during training.

Method: SASS combines three components: (1) Label-free self-supervised gradient scoring to avoid needing candidate ground-truth masks during scoring; (2) Prior-guided category rebalancing with validation-driven feedback to address long-tail imbalance; (3) Stage-adaptive acquisition aligned with model training dynamics to determine when to use model-derived scores. The framework was evaluated on a 3D medical segmentation dataset with 100,000+ samples and 108 anatomical structures.

Result: SASS recovers 98.3% of full-dataset performance with only 40% annotation budget, outperforming BADGE by 5.1 percentage points. It exhibits a "less-is-more" pattern - surpassing full-dataset training at the Hard-group level and for specific structures (pancreas, gallbladder).

Conclusion: Annotation-efficient learning depends not only on which samples are selected, but also on how the annotation budget is distributed across categories and when model-derived scores begin to guide selection. The framework successfully addresses the long-tail problem in dense prediction active learning.

Abstract: Deep learning performance generally improves with increasing training data, yet this scaling is fundamentally constrained by annotation cost in large-scale dense prediction tasks with long-tailed category distributions, where pixel- or voxel-level annotation is prohibitively expensive. We propose SASS (Stage-Adaptive Sample Selection), a stage-adaptive data-selection framework for pool-based active learning in long-tailed dense prediction. SASS combines three components: label-free self-supervised gradient scoring, prior-guided category rebalancing with validation-driven feedback, and stage-adaptive acquisition aligned with model training dynamics. This design avoids candidate ground-truth masks during gradient scoring while making acquisition responsive to long-tail imbalance and evolving representations. We evaluate SASS on a multimodal 3D medical segmentation testbed comprising over 100,000 samples spanning 108 anatomical structures. SASS recovers 98.3% of full-dataset performance with a 40% training-pool annotation budget, outperforming BADGE by 5.1 percentage points. Moreover, SASS exhibits a statistically supported less-is-more pattern, surpassing full-dataset training at the Hard-group level and, at the structure level, for the pancreas and gallbladder. More broadly, SASS shows that annotation-efficient learning depends not only on which samples are selected, but also on how the annotation budget is distributed across categories and when model-derived scores begin to guide selection.

</details>


### [49] [MatchFusion: Explicit-Implicit Instance Matching for Spatio-Temporal Multimodal Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25860)
*Xiaoyu Li,Jiajia Fu,Long Shi,Tianyu Du,Ruihang Li,Xian Wu,Lijun Zhao,Yingtao Zhang,Lining Sun,Ruifeng Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MatchFusion is a learnable instance matching and fusion module for spatio-temporal multimodal autonomous driving. It combines geometric similarity and category consistency for affinity initialization with instance embedding-based refinement, enabling efficient LiDAR-camera and temporal past-current interaction while reducing computational overhead by 55.3% and GPU memory by 39.3%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current methods for instance correspondence in multimodal perception face a trade-off: attention-based methods exploit contextual semantics but require specialized representation alignment and have high computational overhead; association-based methods are efficient but lack contextual evidence for ambiguous matches. This work aims to combine the complementary strengths of both approaches.

Method: MatchFusion initializes pairwise affinities using geometric similarity and category consistency, then selectively refines structurally plausible associations using instance embeddings. The resulting soft matchmap guides a common residual aggregation operator for adaptive information exchange. The method uses multi-view image-plane geometry and motion-compensated BEV geometry as structural priors for spatial LiDAR-camera and temporal past-current interaction respectively.

Result: On nuScenes, MatchFusion achieves consistent perception gains across diverse front-end configurations. Compared with a prior instance-centric fusion method, it achieves higher perception accuracy while reducing FLOPs by 55.3% and GPU memory usage by 39.3%. The matching-fusion module accounts for only 3.7% of total perception latency. Integration with SparseDrive in an E2E framework further improves perception without additional supervision.

Conclusion: Explicit-implicit matching (combining structured association with learned refinement) is an effective and efficient mechanism for spatio-temporal instance interaction in multimodal autonomous driving perception.

Abstract: Sparse instance representations provide a compact interface for spatial LiDAR-camera and temporal past-current interaction in multimodal perception and E2EAD. Effective interaction requires reliable instance correspondences despite geometric discrepancies and heterogeneous semantic representations. Attention-based methods exploit contextual semantics but often require specialized representation alignment, increasing computational overhead. In contrast, association based on structured object states is efficient and interpretable but lacks contextual evidence to resolve ambiguous matches. To combine these complementary strengths, we propose MatchFusion, a learnable instance matching and fusion module for spatio-temporal multimodal autonomous driving. MatchFusion initializes pairwise affinities using geometric similarity and category consistency, then selectively refines structurally plausible associations using instance embeddings. The resulting soft matchmap guides a common residual aggregation operator for adaptive information exchange. This unified matching-fusion formulation supports spatial LiDAR-camera and temporal past-current interaction, using multi-view image-plane geometry and motion-compensated BEV geometry as the respective structural priors. Experiments on nuScenes demonstrate consistent perception gains across diverse front-end configurations. Compared with a prior instance-centric fusion method, the MatchFusion-equipped system achieves higher perception accuracy while reducing FLOPs by 55.3% and GPU memory usage by 39.3%, with the matching-fusion module accounting for only 3.7% of total perception latency. Integrating temporal MatchFusion into SparseDrive further improves perception within an E2E framework without additional supervision. These results establish explicit-implicit matching as an effective and efficient mechanism for spatio-temporal instance interaction.

</details>


### [50] [Delving into Asymmetric Information Dynamics for High-Fidelity Virtual Try-On](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25881)
*Zishu Qin,Zhiyu Jin,Pipei Huang,Hao Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper addresses texture degradation and structural drift in Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for virtual try-on (VTON) by identifying symmetric bidirectional attention as the root cause. They propose RealFit, a framework using Unidirectional Information Flow (UIF) and Decoupled Timestep Modulation (DTM) to preserve garment identity while reducing inference time by ~75% through conditional KV cache.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Virtual try-on requires precise pixel-level fidelity, but mainstream Diffusion Transformers suffer from texture degradation and structural drift due to symmetric interactions in joint-attention mechanisms that allow stochastic noise to corrupt deterministic garment features.

Method: The authors analyze the problem through asymmetric information dynamics using two diagnostic indicators: Conditional Attention Entropy (CAE) for feature unbiasedness and Injected Information Flux (IIF) for injection effectiveness. They propose RealFit framework combining Unidirectional Information Flow (UIF) to isolate garment condition from stochastic noise, and Decoupled Timestep Modulation (DTM) to optimize modulation scale.

Result: The proposed RealFit achieves state-of-the-art fidelity while reducing inference time by approximately 75% through a conditional KV cache enabled by the time-invariant condition branch.

Conclusion: Symmetric bidirectional attention corrupts conditional features and attenuates the conditional signal in VTON tasks. RealFit provides a principled approach to conditional generation with both superior fidelity and efficiency by decoupling the conditional branch from stochastic noise.

Abstract: Virtual try-on (VTON) requires precise pixel-level fidelity, yet mainstream Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) often suffer from texture degradation and structural drift. We identify symmetric interactions in standard joint-attention mechanisms as a source of these failures. Although such interactions support semantic flexibility in general-purpose editing, they allow stochastic noise to corrupt deterministic garment features in VTON. We analyze this problem through asymmetric information dynamics and introduce two diagnostic indicators: Conditional Attention Entropy (CAE) for feature unbiasedness and Injected Information Flux (IIF) for injection effectiveness. Our analysis suggests that symmetric bidirectional attention can corrupt conditional features and attenuate the conditional signal. To address these limitations, we propose RealFit, a framework that combines Unidirectional Information Flow (UIF) with Decoupled Timestep Modulation (DTM). UIF isolates the garment condition from stochastic noise to preserve garment identity, while DTM optimizes the modulation scale to maintain a strong conditional signal. The resulting time-invariant condition branch enables a conditional KV cache that reduces inference time by approximately 75%. RealFit offers a principled approach to conditional generation with state-of-the-art fidelity and efficiency.

</details>


### [51] [BAS-OPD: Budget-Aware Selective On-Policy Self-Distillation for Fine-Grained Multimodal Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25891)
*Zihan Chen,Hengguang Zhou,Yuan Kang,Yiming Zhang,Wenhui Fang,Zenghui Ding,Yining Sun,Cho-Jui Hsieh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BAS-OPD is a budget-aware selective on-policy self-distillation framework for multimodal large language models that reduces teacher supervision costs while maintaining fine-grained visual perception performance by selectively querying informative samples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multimodal large language models struggle with fine-grained visual perception when processing complete images because critical evidence may only appear in local regions. Existing on-policy self-distillation methods require querying the teacher for every rollout, which introduces substantial supervision costs. There is a need to allocate teacher supervision under limited query budgets while maintaining model performance.

Method: BAS-OPD proposes a budget-aware selective OPD framework that: 1) Selects informative samples instead of querying all rollouts while maintaining full-batch student generation; 2) Explores three selection strategies: random, uncertainty-based, and learned utility-based selection; 3) Uses a learned selector that estimates query value from detached rollout statistics and online utility signals (student-teacher agreement and teacher confidence) without additional student forward passes; 4) Only changes training-time supervision allocation while preserving single-pass full-image inference.

Result: Experiments on fine-grained multimodal perception benchmarks demonstrate that BAS-OPD achieves strong performance while substantially reducing teacher supervision costs.

Conclusion: Selective OPD under constrained budgets is effective for fine-grained visual perception tasks. The learned utility-based selection strategy successfully identifies informative samples, allowing significant reduction in teacher supervision costs while maintaining competitive performance.

Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) often struggle with fine-grained visual perception when processing complete images, as critical evidence may only appear in local regions. On-policy self-distillation (OPD) enables transferring privileged visual knowledge from informative views to full-image policies, but querying the teacher for every rollout introduces substantial supervision costs. In this work, we propose BAS-OPD, a budget-aware selective OPD framework that allocates teacher supervision under limited query budgets. Instead of querying all rollouts, BAS-OPD selects informative samples while maintaining full-batch student generation. We explore random, uncertainty-based, and learned utility-based selection strategies, where the learned selector estimates query value from detached rollout statistics and online utility signals derived from student--teacher agreement and teacher confidence without additional student forward passes. BAS-OPD only changes training-time supervision allocation and preserves single-pass full-image inference. Experiments on fine-grained multimodal perception benchmarks demonstrate that BAS-OPD achieves strong performance while substantially reducing teacher supervision costs, highlighting the effectiveness of selective OPD under constrained budgets.

</details>


### [52] [NaCR: Visual Localization via NeRF-aided Camera Ray Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25907)
*Yesheng Zhang,Xiang Dai,Xu Zhao,Chongyang Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes NaCR (NeRF-aided Camera Ray Regression), a unified framework that bridges Neural Radiance Fields (NeRF) and Camera Ray Regression (CRR) to improve visual localization accuracy. The method leverages NeRF's differentiable rendering for data augmentation and closed-loop supervision, achieving competitive accuracy on indoor and outdoor benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Camera Ray Regression (CRR) is a novel visual localization paradigm that maps 2D image patches to 3D camera rays, but its accuracy is limited. The authors observe a complementary duality: CRR performs the inverse mapping of what NeRF does (NeRF renders image patches from camera rays via differentiable ray marching). This insight motivates leveraging NeRF to improve CRR accuracy.

Method: The proposed NaCR framework incorporates three main components: (1) Three simple yet effective enhancements to the CRR baseline; (2) Data augmentation using a pre-trained NeRF to synthesize novel views tailored for patch-level consumption; (3) A closed-loop supervision pipeline exploiting NeRF's differentiability, where photometric rendering errors are back-propagated to optimize predicted camera rays. A two-stage training curriculum is introduced to ensure stable convergence in the highly non-convex image space.

Result: NaCR achieves competitive accuracy on extensive experiments across indoor and outdoor benchmarks. Comprehensive ablation studies validate the efficacy of each proposed component.

Conclusion: The complementary relationship between CRR and NeRF can be effectively leveraged to improve visual localization accuracy. The proposed NaCR framework demonstrates that bridging these two paradigms at the ray level, through data augmentation and differentiable supervision, leads to significant improvements over the CRR baseline.

Abstract: Visual localization (VL) is a fundamental technology for vision applications such as virtual reality. Recently, a novel VL paradigm, Camera Ray Regression (CRR), has emerged, which maps 2D image patches to 3D camera rays, but its accuracy is limited. To improve CRR accuracy, we notice a compelling duality: the inverse of this mapping is inherently performed by the novel view synthesis model, \ie, Neural Radiance Fields (NeRF). While NeRF renders image patches from camera rays via differentiable ray marching, CRR predicts the rays from image patches. Motivated by this complementary relationship, we propose NeRF-aided Camera Ray Regression (NaCR), a unified framework that seamlessly bridges NeRF and CRR at the ray level. First, NaCR incorporates three simple yet effective enhancements into the CRR baseline. Second, leveraging a pre-trained NeRF, NaCR augments the training data by synthesizing novel views tailored for efficient, patch-level consumption. Finally, exploiting the differentiability of NeRF, NaCR forms a closed-loop supervision pipeline where photometric rendering errors are back-propagated to optimize the predicted camera rays. To ensure stable convergence within the highly non-convex image space, we introduce a two-stage training curriculum. Extensive experiments across indoor and outdoor benchmarks demonstrate that NaCR achieves competitive accuracy. Comprehensive ablation studies validate the efficacy of each proposed component.

</details>


### [53] [AT3D-AD: Anomaly Type-Aware 3D Anomaly Detection via Hierarchical Point-Language Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25930)
*Jingyu Zeng,Haoquan Lu,Can Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AT3D-AD is a unified framework for 3D point-cloud anomaly detection that jointly performs detection, localization, and classification. It uses synthetic anomaly generation via parametric functions, aligns global-local representations, and jointly learns semantic and geometric features for precise anomaly type recognition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D point-cloud anomaly detection methods suffer from imprecise localization due to: 1) lack of anomaly supervision, and 2) reliance on single-granularity representations. These limitations prevent accurate defect detection in industrial inspection scenarios.

Method: The paper proposes three key modules: 1) Physics-Driven Parametric Anomaly Synthesis (PDPAS) - generates synthetic anomalies using multiple parametric functions to provide explicit anomaly supervision; 2) Hierarchical Global-Local Anomaly Alignment (HiGLA) - aligns global and local representations within normal and anomalous groups; 3) Semantic-Geometric Anomaly Classification (SGAC) - jointly learns localization and classification to produce spatially precise and type-discriminative anomaly representations.

Result: AT3D-AD achieves state-of-the-art performance on all four benchmarks: Object/Point AUROC of 98.1%/98.9% on Anomaly-ShapeNet and 95.0%/95.2% on Real3D-AD, with 74.2% Macro-F1 for anomaly-type recognition on Real3D-AD.

Conclusion: The proposed AT3D-AD framework successfully addresses the limitations of prior methods by providing anomaly supervision through synthetic anomaly generation, leveraging hierarchical global-local representations, and jointly learning localization and classification. The method demonstrates superior performance across multiple benchmarks, establishing new state-of-the-art results in 3D point-cloud anomaly detection.

Abstract: Detecting and localizing 3D point-cloud defects is essential for industrial inspection. However, existing methods often suffer from imprecise localization due to the lack of anomaly supervision and reliance on single-granularity representations. To address these limitations, we propose Anomaly Type-Aware 3D Anomaly Detection (AT3D-AD), a unified framework for joint detection, localization, and classification. Specifically, we first design the Physics-Driven Parametric Anomaly Synthesis (PDPAS) module employing multiple parametric functions to generate synthetic anomalies, providing explicit anomaly supervision. Then, we propose the Hierarchical Global-Local Anomaly Alignment (HiGLA) module to align global and local representations within the normal and anomalous groups. Finally, we propose the Semantic-Geometric Anomaly Classification (SGAC) module to jointly learn localization and classification, yielding spatially precise and type-discriminative anomaly representations. Extensive experiments establish new state-of-the-art performance on all four benchmarks. AT3D-AD achieves Object/Point AUROC scores of 98.1\%/98.9\% on Anomaly-ShapeNet and 95.0\%/95.2\% on Real3D-AD, while reaching 74.2\% Macro-F1 for anomaly-type recognition on Real3D-AD.

</details>


### [54] [Calibrating Retrieval Geometry: Reliability-Guided Training-Free Aggregation for Visual Place Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25937)
*Xin Li,Zhimin Mao,Shang Wang,Siyuan Duan,Geng Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TFA is a training-free aggregation method for visual place recognition that improves retrieval from frozen visual foundation models (like DINOv2) by using reliability-guided techniques combining cross-codebook agreement, retrieval coverage, and spectral statistics, without requiring place labels or task-specific weight updates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frozen visual foundation models provide transferable features for visual place recognition, but fixed aggregation methods can suppress useful distinctions in new environments where no place labels are available.

Method: TFA combines three components: (1) cross-codebook agreement for residual assignment, (2) retrieval coverage for spectral shaping, and (3) spectral statistics for global-feature fusion. Two variants: database-only TFA (fixes rules before accessing queries) and TFA-C64 (uses 64 disjoint unlabeled target images for calibration).

Result: Database-only TFA improves Recall@1 by 17.39 percentage points on MSLS-val and 9.55 on SPED over AnyLoc. TFA-C64 mitigates calibration failures in driving environments. Across 8 aerial/cross-view protocols, TFA achieves highest Recall@1 in 14/16 backbone-protocol combinations. DINOv2-G-based TFA-C64 reaches 91.46% on Pitts30k and 76.29% on VPAIR.

Conclusion: Reliability-guided aggregation can recover additional retrieval capability from frozen representations, providing a practical baseline for new environments with scarce place supervision.

Abstract: Frozen visual foundation models provide transferable features for visual place recognition, but fixed aggregation can suppress useful distinctions in new environments. We introduce TFA, a reliability-guided, training-free aggregation method requiring neither place labels nor task-specific weight updates. Our key observation is that reproducible retrieval need not be discriminative: independent codebooks can consistently retrieve a few database hubs. TFA combines cross-codebook agreement, retrieval coverage, and spectral statistics to control residual assignment, spectral shaping, and global-feature fusion. Its spectral kernel exactly recovers original descriptor similarity at zero intervention. Database-only TFA fixes its rules before accessing queries; TFA-C64 uses 64 disjoint unlabeled target images to calibrate retrieval for subsequent queries. Across 20 ground protocols with a fixed DINOv2-B backbone and matched resolution, database-only TFA improves Recall@1 over AnyLoc by 17.39 percentage points on MSLS-val and 9.55 on SPED. C64 mitigates failures of database-only calibration in driving environments. Across eight aerial/cross-view protocols, TFA achieves the highest Recall@1 among compared training-free heads in 14 of 16 DINOv2/DINOv3 backbone-protocol combinations. In a separate native-system comparison, DINOv2-G-based TFA-C64 reaches 91.46% Recall@1 on Pitts30k and 76.29% on VPAIR, outperforming the displayed training-free comparators on all five benchmarks. These results show that reliability-guided aggregation can recover additional retrieval capability from frozen representations, providing a practical baseline for new environments with scarce place supervision.

</details>


### [55] [GRIP: Gaussian Rendering as a Cross-Modal Bridge for Image-to-Point Cloud Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25966)
*Karim Slimani,Catherine Achard,Eric Marchand,Brahim Tamadazte*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GRIP is a pose-conditioned refinement framework for pixel-to-point matching and 2D to 3D registration that addresses structural mismatch between grid-based image descriptors and unordered point cloud descriptors through Gaussian feature splatting and pixel-aligned transformers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the structural mismatch between grid-based image descriptors and unordered point cloud descriptors when performing 2D to 3D registration, starting from an initial coarse pose estimate.

Method: GRIP uses Gaussian feature splatting to softly render learned 3D point features onto the image grid, creating a rendered point-derived feature map. This is fused with image features via a pixel-aligned transformer, enabling visual semantic and geometric cues to interact in a shared 2D representation. The refined features are decoded and propagated to finer resolutions for dense correspondence estimation and final pose refinement.

Result: Experiments on RGB-D Scenes V2 and 7 Scenes demonstrate state-of-the-art inlier ratio and competitive registration recall, with stronger performance under stricter evaluation thresholds.

Conclusion: GRIP effectively addresses the structural mismatch problem in pixel-to-point matching and achieves superior performance in pose refinement tasks, particularly showing strength under stricter evaluation thresholds.

Abstract: This paper introduces GRIP, a pose-conditioned refinement framework for pixel-to-point matching and 2D to 3D registration. Given an initial coarse pose estimate, GRIP addresses the structural mismatch between grid based image descriptors and unordered point cloud descriptors by softly rendering learned 3D point features onto the image grid through Gaussian feature splatting. The rendered point derived feature map is then fused with image features by a pixel aligned transformer, enabling visual semantic and geometric cues to interact in a shared 2D representation. The refined features are decoded and propagated to finer resolutions for dense correspondence estimation and final pose refinement. Experiments on RGB D Scenes V2 and 7 Scenes demonstrate state of the art inlier ratio and competitive registration recall, with stronger performance under stricter evaluation thresholds.

</details>


### [56] [NAWE: Digital Watermarking with Neural-Assisted Watermark Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25972)
*Roman Chaban,Vitaliy Kinakh,Lilian Rouzaire,Slava Voloshynovskiy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NAWE combines explicit signal-processing watermarking with a pretrained neural host predictor for watermark extraction. It uses a periodic, perceptually masked watermark carrier for synchronization, Polar coding for redundancy, and denoising followed by subtraction for watermark extraction, with a frozen non-adaptive denoiser.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Improve watermark extraction robustness by combining neural host prediction with explicit signal-processing watermarking, while maintaining a non-adaptive (frozen) denoiser without watermark-specific training.

Method: Uses periodic watermark carrier with perceptual masking for synchronization, Polar coding for redundancy, and compares four denoising methods (Wiener, BM3D, DRUNet, GS-DRUNet) as host estimators. Extraction is performed via denoising followed by subtraction.

Result: NAWE achieves the lowest geometric and photometric class BER compared to TrustMark, SSL Watermarking, PixelSeal, and WAM, with strong message recovery. However, filtering and noise remain limitations due to the non-adaptive watermark extractor selection.

Conclusion: The neural-assisted approach shows promising results but faces limitations from filtering and noise, consistent with the non-adaptive selection of the watermark extractor. The comparison accounts for different payloads and coding schemes across systems.

Abstract: NAWE (Neural-Assisted Watermark Extraction) combines an explicit signal-processing watermarking construction with a pretrained neural host predictor. A periodic, perceptually masked watermark carrier provides synchronization, Polar coding supplies redundancy, and denoising followed by subtraction extracts the embedded watermark. The denoiser remains frozen, without watermark-specific training. A one-factor-at-a-time study compares Wiener, BM3D, DRUNet, and GS-DRUNet host estimators. Comparisons with TrustMark, SSL Watermarking, PixelSeal, and WAM show NAWE's lowest geometric and photometric class BER and strong message recovery, while filtering and noise remain limitations consistent with the non-adaptive selection of the watermark extractor. The comparison retains the systems' different payloads and coding.

</details>


### [57] [Faithful Faithfulness Evaluations: Challenges & Pitfalls Learned from a Breast MRI Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25978)
*Peachapong Poolpol,Henrik H. J. Detjen,Eike Petersen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates the reliability of saliency maps for explaining Vision Transformer-based breast MRI classifiers, revealing that method rankings vary based on evaluation design and highlighting the need to distinguish between class-specific and class-agnostic explanations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Saliency maps are widely used to explain deep learning predictions in medical imaging, but visually plausible explanations may not reflect a model's true decision process and could mislead clinicians. This raises concerns about the trustworthiness of current explainability methods in clinical settings.

Method: The study used a Vision Transformer-based breast MRI classifier trained on the ODELIA Breast MRI Challenge dataset. Seven saliency methods were evaluated: Last-layer Attention, Attention Rollout, Grad-SAM, Gradient Attention Rollout, GMAR, Grad-CAM, and HiResCAM. The researchers investigated two key challenges in perturbation-based faithfulness evaluation: (1) the impact of different perturbation strategies (intensity-based vs. transformer-based attention masking), and (2) the need to distinguish between class-specific and class-agnostic explanations. They introduced non-class-specific variants of gradient-based methods for fair comparison.

Result: Method rankings depend strongly on the perturbation strategy, varying across intensity-based perturbations and transformer-based attention masking. Grad-CAM and Gradient Attention Rollout consistently emerged as the strongest class-specific methods, though their relative ranking depended on the evaluation design.

Conclusion: Current saliency-based explainability approaches have important limitations. There is a need for more robust and standardized evaluation frameworks to ensure trustworthy clinical AI systems.

Abstract: Saliency maps are widely used to explain deep learning predictions in medical imaging, yet visually plausible explanations do not necessarily reflect a model's true decision process and may therefore mislead clinicians. We investigate this problem using a Vision Transformer-based breast MRI classifier trained on the ODELIA Breast MRI Challenge dataset and evaluate multiple saliency methods, including Last-layer Attention, Attention Rollout, Grad-SAM, Gradient Attention Rollout, GMAR, Grad-CAM, and HiResCAM. Our study highlights two often-overlooked challenges in perturbation-based faithfulness evaluation. First, method rankings depend strongly on the perturbation strategy, varying across intensity-based perturbations and transformer-based attention masking. Second, benchmarking saliency methods requires distinguishing between class-specific and class-agnostic explanations. To enable fair comparisons, we introduce non-class-specific variants of gradient-based methods and evaluate both settings separately. Across protocols, Grad-CAM and Gradient Attention Rollout consistently emerged as the strongest class-specific methods, although their relative ranking depended on the evaluation design. These findings expose important limitations of current saliency-based explainability approaches and highlight the need for more robust and standardized evaluation frameworks for trustworthy clinical AI systems.

</details>


### [58] [EMERGE: Resolution-Agnostic Point Cloud Generation with Equivariant Graph-Based Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26039)
*Ilias Mitsouras,Nikolaos Chaidos,Giorgos Stamou,Athanasios Voulodimos*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EMERGE is the first SE(3)-equivariant graph-based diffusion model for resolution-agnostic point cloud generation, addressing limitations of existing Transformer/VAE approaches that ignore continuous 3D spatial topologies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing generative approaches for point clouds predominantly rely on Transformers and VAEs, which frequently ignore the continuous, non-grid topologies inherent to 3D spaces. While graph-based structures have shown benefits in discriminative vision tasks, they remain absent from 3D generative modeling. Additionally, existing methods suffer from rigid resolution dependencies.

Method: EMERGE introduces an equivariant multi-scale graph neural network (GNN) backbone that maintains SE(3) equivariance (preserving continuous spatial symmetries) and enables zero-shot inference at multiple, arbitrary spatial resolutions through a resolution-agnostic architecture.

Result: EMERGE achieves State-of-the-Art generation quality across standard metrics for point cloud generation, while demonstrating significantly faster training convergence due to strong inherent geometric inductive biases.

Conclusion: This work demonstrates that graph-based architectures with proper equivariance constraints can effectively address key limitations in 3D point cloud generation, offering improved quality, resolution flexibility, and training efficiency compared to existing baseline methods.

Abstract: Point cloud generation has emerged as a crucial task for accurately capturing and reproducing the complexity of the physical world. However, existing generative approaches, predominantly relying on Transformers and Variational Autoencoders (VAEs), frequently ignore the continuous, non-grid topologies inherent to 3D spaces. Although the integration of graph-based structures has yielded significant benefits in related discriminative vision tasks, such geometric architectures remain noticeably absent from 3D generative modeling. To address this gap, we introduce EMERGE (Equivariant Multi-scale GNN for Resolution-agnostic point cloud GEneration), the first fully $SE(3)$-equivariant graph-based diffusion backbone explicitly designed to generate point clouds while preserving continuous spatial symmetries. Our framework bypasses the rigid resolution dependencies of standard generative pipelines, enabling zero-shot inference at multiple, arbitrary spatial resolutions. Extensive empirical evaluations demonstrate that EMERGE achieves State-of-the-Art generation quality across standard metrics, while the strong inherent geometric inductive biases enable significantly faster training convergence compared to existing baseline methods.

</details>


### [59] [CricRAG: Retrieval Augmented Vision-Language Models for Personalized Cricket Coaching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26056)
*Agamdeep Singh,Sujit PB,Mayank Vatsa*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CricRAG is a retrieval-augmented framework that enables Vision-Language Models to provide developmentally appropriate feedback for cricket coaching by retrieving similar-but-better technique examples as reference points, addressing the limitation that VLMs implicitly compare against professional standards.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for sports coaching face a fundamental limitation: they implicitly compare against professional standards, making feedback impractical for developing players. This work aims to align VLMs with skill-appropriate benchmarks to provide personalized, developmentally appropriate feedback similar to human coaching practices.

Method: The framework includes: (1) a labeled dataset of 288 cricket technique videos across multiple skill levels, (2) an efficient motion retrieval pipeline using contrastive learning achieving 78% top-3 retrieval accuracy, (3) a frame sampling technique to reduce inference costs, and (4) a retrieval-augmented approach that retrieves similar-but-better techniques as reference points to guide VLM feedback generation.

Result: The motion retrieval pipeline achieves 78% top-3 retrieval accuracy. The retrieval-augmented approach significantly improves feedback alignment with coaching principles, achieving 94% agreement with professional assessments compared to 67% without retrieval context.

Conclusion: CricRAG successfully aligns VLMs with skill-appropriate benchmarks for personalized cricket coaching. The retrieval-augmented approach substantially improves feedback quality, achieving 94% agreement with professional assessments (vs. 67% baseline), demonstrating that retrieving similar-but-better techniques as references enables developmentally appropriate feedback that mirrors human coaching practices.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) offer promising capabilities for automated sports coaching but face a fundamental limitation: they implicitly compare against professional standards, making their feedback impractical for developing players. We present CricRAG, a retrieval-augmented framework that aligns VLMs with skill-appropriate benchmarks for personalized cricket coaching. Our key insight is that by retrieving similar-but-better techniques as reference points, we can guide VLMs to provide developmentally appropriate feedback that mirrors human coaching practices. We contribute: (1) a labelled dataset of 288 cricket technique videos spanning multiple skill levels, (2) an efficient motion retrieval pipeline using contrastive learning that achieves 78% top-3 retrieval accuracy, (3) a frame sampling technique that reduces inference costs, and (4) a retrieval-augmented approach that significantly improves feedback alignment with coaching principles, achieving up to 94% agreement with professional assessments compared to 67% without retrieval context.

</details>


### [60] [SPEANet: Structural Prior Enhanced Attention Network for Parameter-Efficient Remote Sensing Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26064)
*Wei Lu,Junjie Li,Feifei Sang,Si-Bao Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SPEANet is a parameter-efficient backbone for remote sensing object detection (RSOD) that integrates fixed structural operators through stage-specific prior extraction and context-conditioned response modulation. It assigns smoothed contour and multi-order directional modeling to shallow features, while using approximation-detail interaction in deeper stages, with learned spatial gates for response regulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RSOD requires compact backbones capable of preserving weak geometric cues under extreme scale variation and background clutter. Fixed structural operators provide complementary contour and frequency responses without learnable coefficients, but direct injection amplifies content-irrelevant textures, and uniform operator design fails to match stage-specific representation requirements.

Method: SPEANet integrates fixed operators through: (1) Stage-specific prior extraction and context-conditioned response modulation; (2) Smoothed contour and multi-order directional modeling for shallow, high-resolution features; (3) Compact approximation-detail interaction mechanism in deeper stages; (4) Learned spatial gates to regulate prior responses before residual fusion.

Result: With Oriented R-CNN, SPEANet achieves 78.55% mAP on DOTA-v1.0, 72.24% mAP on DOTA-v1.5, and 67.30% mAP on DIOR-R using 23.0M total parameters (5.97M backbone). Experiments on five benchmarks with seven detection frameworks demonstrate favorable accuracy-parameter trade-off.

Conclusion: SPEANet successfully addresses the challenge of integrating fixed structural operators in RSOD backbones through stage-specific design and learned gating, achieving state-of-the-art performance with only 5.97M backbone parameters while maintaining strong generalization across multiple detection frameworks.

Abstract: Remote sensing object detection (RSOD) requires compact backbones capable of preserving weak geometric cues under extreme scale variation and background clutter. Fixed structural operators provide complementary contour and frequency responses without introducing learnable operator coefficients. However, directly injecting these responses can amplify content-irrelevant textures, while applying a uniform operator design across the hierarchy may be poorly matched to stage-specific representation requirements. We propose the Structural Prior Enhanced Attention Network (SPEANet), a parameter-efficient RSOD backbone that integrates fixed operators through stage-specific prior extraction and context-conditioned response modulation. SPEANet assigns smoothed contour and multi-order directional modeling to shallow, high-resolution features, while employing a compact approximation-detail interaction mechanism in deeper stages. Learned spatial gates regulate the resulting prior responses before residual fusion. Experiments on five benchmarks, together with evaluations across seven detection frameworks on DOTA-v1.0, achieve a favorable accuracy-parameter trade-off. With Oriented R-CNN, SPEANet achieves 78.55\% mAP on DOTA-v1.0, 72.24\% mAP on DOTA-v1.5, and 67.30\% mAP on DIOR-R using 23.0M total parameters, including a 5.97M-parameter backbone.

</details>


### [61] [Cellular-Communication-Level Interpretability for Pathology Foundation Models via Graph Distillation on Microenvironment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26073)
*Yuxiang Xiao,Zhiwei Chen,Dan Dai,Wei Li,Tianyang Zhang,Yakun Ju,Yang Hu,Kaixiang Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Graph-Interpreter (G-Interp) is a graph-distillation framework that adds interpretability to frozen pathology foundation models (PFMs) by learning cellular communication-level explanations through graph neural networks, without modifying the original teacher model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pathology foundation models provide strong tile-level representations but lack interpretability at the cellular and microenvironmental scales that are critical for clinical reasoning. There is a need to understand how PFMs encode microenvironmental context.

Method: The method uses a graph-based approach: (1) Segment cells as graph nodes from tissue tiles; (2) Construct microenvironment graphs based on spatial adjacency; (3) Train GNN students to distill PFM embeddings while learning attention-based message passing; (4) Extract node- and edge-level importances as interpretability signals; (5) Use an auxiliary visual student to handle graph abstraction imperfections and condition graph message passing. Evaluation uses instance masks to map graph-selected evidence back to images and measures teacher sensitivity under targeted vs non-target occlusions.

Result: G-Interp produces highly scalable, microenvironment-aware explanations while maintaining competitive predictive performance across multiple histopathology tasks. The method provides fine-grained explanations of how PFMs encode cellular communication patterns.

Conclusion: The proposed framework successfully adds interpretability to frozen PFMs at the cellular level without compromising their predictive performance, enabling better understanding of microenvironmental context encoding in pathology AI models.

Abstract: Pathology foundation models (PFMs) provide strong tile-level representations but remain difficult to interpret at the cellular and microenvironmental scales that underpin clinical reasoning. We introduce Graph-Interpreter (G-Interp), a graph-distillation framework that equips a frozen PFM teacher with a cellular-communication-level "plug-in" interpreter, without modifying the teacher. For each tile, we segment cells as graph nodes and construct a microenvironment graph based on spatial adjacency. Graph neural network (GNN) students distil the PFM embedding, whilst learning attention-based message passing that yields node- and edge-level importances. We interpret these importances as cell-cell communication evidence, providing fine-grained explanations of how PFMs encode microenvironmental context. To stabilise distillation when graph abstraction is imperfect, we employ a lightweight auxiliary student to supply complementary visual cues and condition graph message passing, while keeping the primary interpretability signal graph-derived. We evaluate explanation faithfulness by mapping graph-selected evidence back to the image using instance masks and measuring teacher sensitivity under targeted vs non-target occlusions. Across multiple histopathology tasks, G-Interp produces highly scalable, microenvironment-aware explanations, while maintaining competitive predictive performance.

</details>


### [62] [ToW3D: Consistency-aware Interactive Point-based Mesh Editing on GANs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26078)
*Haixu Song,Fangfu Liu,Chenyu Zhang,Yueqi Duan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ToW3D enables precise and consistent control over 3D GANs by introducing a Tug-of-War competition between shape deformation and appearance consistency, using a "drag locally, shove globally" pipeline with structure adaptation and semantic preservation modules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing point-based GAN editing methods (DragGAN, GANWarping) designed for 2D images fail when applied to 3D generators because 3D generators have weaker generalization ability due to limited training data, leading to drastic changes in global appearance when editing local areas of meshes.

Method: The proposed method uses a "drag locally, shove globally" pipeline with two iterative optimization steps: (1) pulling the point towards the target, and (2) pushing the structure and semantics back to the source. It includes a structure adaptation module to preserve basic geometric properties and a semantic preservation module to maintain semantic similarity across different views.

Result: Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that ToW3D outperforms prior methods in terms of appearance consistency and fidelity, especially under large deformations.

Conclusion: The Tug-of-War competition mechanism between shape deformation and appearance consistency effectively addresses the appearance distortion issue in 3D GAN editing, providing superior control over 3D generative models.

Abstract: In this paper, we propose ToW3D that enables precise and consistent control over 3D generative adversarial networks (GANs) with the Tug-of-War competition between shape deformation and appearance consistency. Existing point-based GAN editing methods such as DragGAN and GANWarping have yielded impressive performance for 2D image manipulation. However, as 3D generators present weaker generalization ability compared with 2D due to limited training data, they would suffer from drastic changes in global appearance when editing local areas of meshes. To address this, we design a pipeline of ``drag locally, shove globally'', which iteratively performs two optimization steps: 1) pull the point towards the target, and 2) push the structure and semantics back to the source. Specifically, we design a structure adaption module based on structure which guarantees the preservation of basic geometric properties, and a semantic preservation module that maintains semantic similarity across different views. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate superiority of our ToW3D approach over prior methods in terms of appearance consistency and fidelity especially under large deformations.

</details>


### [63] [BDSLI: A hybrid CNN-Transformer model for Bengali Sign Language interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26088)
*Abir Bin Yousuf,Muhammad Iqbal Hossain*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel hybrid CNN-Transformer architecture is proposed for Bengali Sign Language Recognition (SLR), achieving 98.65% test accuracy on a custom dataset of 62 sign words, with deployment in a web application.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bengali SLR has limited progress, and this study addresses this gap by introducing a CNN-Transformer hybrid model for isolated sign word recognition and sentence generation - a novel combination for Bengali SLR tasks.

Method: A custom video dataset was developed with 62 distinct Bengali sign words (250 samples/class) plus a separate test set. The CNN-Transformer model was compared against baseline models including CNN-LSTM and standalone TCN.

Result: The CNN-Transformer achieved 99.58% training accuracy, 99.48% validation accuracy, and 98.65% test accuracy, outperforming all comparative and baseline models.

Conclusion: The hybrid CNN-Transformer architecture demonstrates superior performance for Bengali SLR, and the trained model was successfully deployed in a web application for real-world validation.

Abstract: This study introduces a novel hybrid CNN-Transformer architecture to address the limited progress in Bengali SLR, focusing on isolated sign word recognition and sentence generation. This specific model combination is new to Bengali SLR tasks. A custom video dataset was developed, featuring 62 distinct Bengali sign words (250 samples/class), along with a separate test dataset. The CNN-Transformer model demonstrated superior performance against all comparative and baseline models (e.g., CNN-LSTM, standalone TCN), achieving a 99.58% training accuracy (99.48% validation) and a 98.65% test accuracy. The trained model was subsequently deployed in a web application for real-world validation.

</details>


### [64] [Match One, Learn with Graph: One-to-Graph Query Collaboration with Backward Sharing for Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26092)
*Wenxiao Fan,Jingling Fu,Luohang Liu,Lichen Ma,Yu He,Zhiyang Yu,Weishan Bi,Junshi Huang,Yan Li,Gu Simiu,Kan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BS-O2G is a plug-in method for Detection Transformers (DETRs) that addresses "query knowledge fragmentation" by building a sparse prediction-aware graph from decoded features, boxes, and class distributions to enable query collaboration in feature and optimization spaces, while preserving the original one-to-one matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In DETRs with one-to-one (O2O) matching, the strongest classification, center, scale, and overlap evidence for an object is distributed across multiple queries, but only the matched query receives positive supervision. This "query knowledge fragmentation" leaves valuable evidence-bearing queries unused.

Method: BS-O2G consists of two components: (1) One-to-Graph (O2G) calibration - propagates relative messages over a sparse prediction-aware graph built from decoded features, boxes, and class distributions to consolidate query evidence in the forward pass; (2) Backward Sharing (BS) - reuses a transposed detached adjacency to route gradients across persistent query basis vectors without changing decoder input. The method preserves the original O2O matcher, positive labels, and objective.

Result: Experiments across diverse DETR methods, backbones, COCO, and CrowdHuman show consistent gains and faster convergence with negligible parameter/FLOP growth and modest runtime overhead.

Conclusion: Graph-based query collaboration serves as an effective alternative to expanding positive assignments (one-to-many supervision) for improving DETR performance.

Abstract: One-to-one (O2O) matching enables Detection Transformers (DETRs) to perform end-to-end set prediction by assigning each object to a single positive query. However, the strongest classification, center, scale, and overlap evidence for an object is often distributed across multiple queries. This mismatch leaves only the matched owner positively supervised for the object, while other evidence-bearing queries receive no box target for it. We term this query knowledge fragmentation. To exploit such complementary evidence without one-to-many supervision, we propose BS-O2G, a plug-in that builds a sparse prediction-aware graph from decoded features, boxes, and class distributions to organize query collaboration in feature and optimization spaces while preserving the original O2O matcher, positive labels, and objective. One-to-Graph (O2G) calibration propagates relative messages over this graph to consolidate query evidence in the forward pass, whereas Backward Sharing (BS) reuses its transposed detached adjacency to route gradients across persistent query basis vectors without changing the decoder input in the forward pass. Experiments across diverse DETR methods, backbones, COCO, and CrowdHuman show consistent gains and faster convergence with negligible parameter/FLOP growth and modest runtime overhead, supporting graph-based query collaboration as an alternative to expanding positive assignments.

</details>


### [65] [RECAP: Relation Evidence Calibration for Detecting Spatial Relation Hallucinations in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26093)
*Feixiang Liu,Qiang Qiu,Qingyang Li,Hui Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RECAP is a relation-evidence calibration framework for vision-language models that detects when model answers to spatial relation questions lack visual support. It compares image-conditioned likelihoods for claims, contradictions, and supports to compute rejection risk, outperforming confidence-only and visual contrast baselines across multiple benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision-language models can confidently answer spatial relation questions incorrectly when the image doesn't support their answer. The paper addresses this overconfidence problem by formulating "relation-grounded selective prediction" - auditing whether answers have visual support rather than treating uncertainty as evidence.

Method: RECAP compares image-conditioned likelihoods for three components: (1) a claim/answer, (2) semantic contradictions, and (3) optional one-sided supports. These "witnesses" are converted into an answer-conditioned rejection risk. A calibration-only gate preserves confidence as a veto when confidence is demonstrably informative, otherwise deploys relation evidence alone.

Result: Across 20 group/image-disjoint splits, RECAP lowers H-FPR@80 by 2.0-17.9 points on VSR and raises Acc@80 by 3.0, 8.6, and 12.6 points on What'sUp for Qwen3-VL-8B, InternVL3.5-8B, and LLaVA-1.5-7B. It outperforms matched VCD-style visual contrast on all four primary metrics in all six settings.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates a consistent operating principle: structured counterevidence complements certainty when confidence is misaligned, while the gate retains confidence when it is already useful. The approach shows successful transfer to VSR fallback, GSR-Bench, and supervised control settings.

Abstract: Vision-language models can answer spatial relation questions confidently even when the image supports an incompatible relation. We formulate relation-grounded selective prediction: accept or reject an already-produced yes/no answer by auditing its visual support, rather than treating uncertainty as evidence. RECAP, our relation-evidence calibration framework, compares image-conditioned likelihoods for a claim, its semantic contradictions, and optional one-sided supports, then converts these witnesses into an answer-conditioned rejection risk. A calibration-only gate preserves confidence as a veto when confidence is demonstrably informative and otherwise deploys relation evidence alone. Across 20 group/image-disjoint splits, RECAP lowers H-FPR@80 over confidence by between 2.0 and 17.9 points on VSR and raises Acc@80 by 3.0, 8.6, and 12.6 points on What'sUp for Qwen3-VL-8B, InternVL3.5-8B, and LLaVA-1.5-7B. It outperforms matched VCD-style visual contrast on all four primary metrics in all six settings. Full-pool VSR fallback, target-ranked GSR-Bench transfer, equal-budget supervised controls, and two additional checkpoints show a consistent operating principle: structured counterevidence complements certainty when confidence is misaligned, while the gate retains confidence when it is already useful.

</details>


### [66] [Test-time Reinforcement Learning for Anomalous Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26099)
*Huining Li,Yuxiang Duan,Jiyang Tan,Qian Li,MingCai Chen,Jian Zhang,Xingdong Sheng,Yuntao Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel test-time reinforcement learning framework for anomalous video understanding using Video-LLMs. The method addresses three key challenges: unreliable pseudo-labels, binary reward limitations, and collapsed group-relative advantages in unanimous rollout groups. It introduces dual-query consistency filtering, entropy-aware consensus reward, and virtual negative anchor mechanism. Results show significant improvements on VAU-Bench, with accuracy on ECVA subset improving from 75.81% to 90.00%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Video-LLMs have limited zero-shot capabilities for anomalous video understanding due to insufficient adaptation to diverse anomaly patterns and evolving environments. Test-time reinforcement learning offers a solution but faces challenges: unreliable pseudo-labels when consensus is weak, binary rewards failing to capture uncertainty, and collapsed group-relative advantages in unanimous rollout groups.

Method: The framework introduces three novel components: (1) Dual-query consistency filtering to retain reliable samples through semantic equivalence queries; (2) Entropy-aware consensus reward combining answer agreement with generation uncertainty for reward estimation; (3) Virtual negative anchor mechanism to create reward variation in unanimous rollout groups, preserving effective group-relative optimization signals.

Result: Experiments on VAU-Bench demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms compared frozen and supervised baselines. The most significant gains are on the ECVA subset of VAU-Bench with thinking, where accuracy improves from 75.81% to 90.00% relative to the frozen backbone.

Conclusion: The test-time reinforcement learning framework with dual-query consistency filtering, entropy-aware consensus reward, and virtual negative anchor mechanism effectively addresses the challenges in applying RL to anomalous video understanding, achieving substantial performance improvements without requiring additional human annotations.

Abstract: Anomalous video understanding aims to identify abnormal events in videos and interpret their semantic meanings beyond simple anomaly detection. Recent video large language models (Video-LLMs) have demonstrated promising zero-shot capabilities for this task, yet their performance remains limited due to insufficient adaptation to diverse anomaly patterns and evolving environments. Test-time reinforcement learning offers a promising solution by enabling models to improve through self-generated feedback signals without requiring additional human annotations. However, applying it to anomalous video understanding remains challenging due to three issues: (1) generated pseudo-labels can be unreliable when consensus is weak; (2) binary reward designs fail to capture uncertainty in model generations, resulting in ineffective optimization signals; and (3) unanimous rollout groups receive identical rewards, causing group-relative advantages to collapse and eliminating effective policy-gradient signals. To address these challenges, we present a novel test-time reinforcement learning framework for anomalous video understanding by introducing dual-query consistency filtering, an entropy-aware consensus reward, and a virtual negative anchor mechanism. The framework retains reliable samples through consistency across semantically equivalent queries, combines answer agreement with generation uncertainty for reward estimation, and introduces a virtual negative anchor to create reward variation in unanimous rollout groups, thereby preserving effective group-relative optimization signals. Experiments on VAU-Bench show that our method outperforms the compared frozen and supervised baselines. The gains are most pronounced on the ECVA subset of VAU-Bench with thinking, where accuracy improves from 75.81% to 90.00% relative to the frozen backbone.

</details>


### [67] [MIAR: Medical Image Super-Resolution With Autoregressive Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26103)
*Fang Li,Yinglong Li,Hongyu Wu,Yang Gao,Minwei Zhao,Aimin Hao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MIAR proposes a multi-scale autoregressive framework for medical image super-resolution, reformulating SR as a conditional and progressive next-scale prediction task. It incorporates a Scale-Adaptive Structural Decoder for structural fidelity and hierarchical beam search to mitigate recursive error accumulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MISR methods face a critical trade-off: diffusion-based methods suffer from high inference latency and compromised structural fidelity, while regression-based models produce over-smoothed results lacking perceptual realism.

Method: MIAR uses a multi-scale autoregressive framework that reformulates super-resolution as a conditional and progressive next-scale prediction task. It is augmented with a Scale-Adaptive Structural Decoder to ensure structural fidelity, and integrates a hierarchical beam search strategy during inference to mitigate recursive error accumulation.

Result: MIAR establishes new state-of-the-art benchmarks with a 7.86% improvement in perceptual metric MUSIQ compared with the state of the art, while delivering a 2.02x speedup over diffusion-based methods.

Conclusion: MIAR successfully addresses the trade-off between perceptual quality and inference speed in medical image super-resolution, achieving superior fidelity and efficiency.

Abstract: Medical Image Super-Resolution (MISR) aims to enhance spatial resolution without requiring hardware modifications. Although deep learning has yielded promising results, existing paradigms face a critical trade-off: diffusion-based methods suffer from prohibitive inference latency and compromised structural fidelity, whereas regression-based models typically produce over-smoothed results that lack perceptual realism. To address these limitations, we propose MIAR, which reformulates super-resolution as a conditional and progressive next-scale prediction task through a multi-scale autoregressive framework. To ensure structural fidelity, we augment the autoregressive backbone with a Scale-Adaptive Structural Decoder. Furthermore, we integrate a hierarchical beam search strategy during inference to mitigate the recursive error accumulation inherent in autoregressive generation, a phenomenon that is especially pronounced in medical images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MIAR establishes new state-of-the-art benchmarks while maintaining superior fidelity. Notably, our framework achieves a 7.86% improvement in the perceptual metric MUSIQ compared with the state of the art, while simultaneously delivering a 2.02x speedup over diffusion-based methods.

</details>


### [68] [Vorch-Human: Unified Multi-Task Human-Centric Generation via Long-Horizon Continuation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26117)
*Yang Ding,Haoran Yu,Xin Ma,Yulei Lu,Menglin Han,Yaole Wang,Siqian Yang,Gang Yue,Kaihao Zhang,Yaohui Wang,Lin Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Vorch-Human is a unified human-centric audio-visual generation framework that addresses multiple related tasks (speech-driven animation, voice-to-video generation, scene synthesis) using a single model built on a dual-stream audio-video diffusion transformer. It uses clean condition tokens, task embeddings, and a two-level data pipeline for training. The system also supports long-form generation through latent prefix techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing systems solve related human-centric audio-visual tasks (animating from speech, generating speech+video from voice, synthesizing from appearance+voice) with separate models, even though they share the same target modalities and differ mainly in conditioning inputs. This fragmentation motivates a unified framework.

Method: 1) Dual-stream audio-video diffusion transformer with clean condition-audio and condition-video token groups
2) Per-token task embeddings, temporal position types, condition masks, and shared multimodal prompt encoder
3) Two-level data pipeline: Level 1 - multi-modal analysis (speech recognition, vocal separation, face detection, speaker clustering); Level 2 - cross-clip identity linking and reference mining
4) Long-form adaptation via clean latent prefix training and frozen-prefix recurrence at inference

Result: Experiments on short and five-minute generation demonstrate strong identity preservation, audio-visual synchronization, and temporal stability.

Conclusion: Vorch-Human successfully unifies multiple human-centric audio-visual generation tasks into a single framework, achieving strong performance on identity preservation, synchronization, and temporal stability while supporting long-form content generation.

Abstract: Human-centric audio-visual generation spans several closely related tasks: animating a person from driving speech, jointly generating speech and video from a voice reference, and synthesizing a scene from paired appearance and voice references. Existing systems commonly solve these tasks with separate models, even though they share the same target modalities and differ mainly in which observations are provided as conditions. We present Vorch-Human, a unified human-centric generation framework built on a dual-stream audio-video diffusion transformer. Vorch-Human augments the conventional noisy audio/noisy video interface with clean condition-audio and condition-video token groups. Per-token task embeddings, temporal position types, condition masks, and a shared multimodal prompt encoder allow driving speech, timbre examples, first frames, and subject images to be expressed within one model. To supply the supervision required by this interface, we develop a two-level data pipeline. Level 1 analyzes each clip with speech recognition, vocal separation, face detection and tracking, active-speaker and synchronization models, audio/visual speaker clustering, and multimodal caption correction; it produces subject-indexed speech, appearance, and timbre annotations. Level 2 links the same person across clips from a common source video and mines identity- and outfit-consistent reference images after face, body, quality, pose, and vision-language verification. Finally, we adapt Vorch-Human to long-form audio-driven generation by training with clean latent prefixes and using the same frozen-prefix recurrence at inference. Each segment contributes only its newly generated suffix, reducing boundary discontinuity and long-horizon identity drift. Experiments on short and five-minute generation demonstrate strong identity preservation, audio-visual synchronization, and temporal stability.

</details>


<div id='cs.SE'></div>

# cs.SE [[Back]](#toc)

### [69] [TRACTOR Benchmark for Evaluating C to Rust Translators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25121)
*Hamed Okhravi,Brandt Ogden,Noah Luther,Ian McQuoid,Howard Mak,Nathan Burow*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: This report describes the TRACTOR benchmark developed by MIT Lincoln Laboratory to systematically evaluate C-to-Rust translation tools. The benchmark includes progressively challenging test batteries and milestone projects, along with evaluation infrastructure and metrics for assessing correctness, safety, idiomaticity, and performance of translated code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Memory-safety vulnerabilities in C/C++ codebases present significant security risks. The DARPA TRACTOR program aims to develop scalable techniques for automatically translating legacy C code to memory-safe Rust. However, there was no standardized benchmark to systematically assess the quality of C-to-Rust translation tools, which created a need for comprehensive evaluation methodology.

Method: MIT Lincoln Laboratory developed a standardized benchmark with: (1) progressively challenging test batteries of increasing complexity, (2) larger milestone projects for comprehensive assessment, (3) supporting evaluation infrastructure, and (4) metrics covering correctness, safety, idiomaticity, and performance. The benchmark and infrastructure are publicly available.

Result: The result is a publicly available benchmark and evaluation framework that enables systematic assessment of C-to-Rust translation tools across multiple dimensions: code correctness, memory safety guarantees, Rust idiomaticity, and runtime performance. This provides a standardized way to compare different translation approaches.

Conclusion: The TRACTOR benchmark provides the research community with a standardized tool for evaluating C-to-Rust translation technologies. By making the benchmark publicly available, DARPA and MIT Lincoln Laboratory support broader development and improvement of automated translation tools, ultimately contributing to modernizing critical software systems with memory-safe languages.

Abstract: Memory-safety vulnerabilities remain a persistent source of security risk in critical software, much of which is implemented in memory-unsafe languages such as C and C++. Recent advances in programming languages, program analysis, and artificial intelligence have created new opportunities to modernize these legacy systems through automated translation to memory-safe languages such as Rust. The DARPA Translating All C to Rust (TRACTOR) program seeks to develop scalable techniques for translating large C codebases into safe, performant, and maintainable Rust. MIT Lincoln Laboratory serves as the program's independent test and evaluation organization and has developed a standardized benchmark for systematically assessing C-to-Rust translation tools. This report describes the TRACTOR benchmark, including progressively challenging test batteries and larger milestone projects, as well as the supporting evaluation infrastructure and metrics for assessing correctness, safety, idiomaticity, and performance. The benchmark and associated evaluation infrastructure are publicly available to support the broader development and evaluation of C-to-Rust translation technologies.

</details>


### [70] [TAILOR: Template-Preserving Augmentation for Long-Tailed Log Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25261)
*Sepideh Hodaeian,Zhenhao Li,An Ran Chen*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: This paper addresses the challenge of parsing rare log groups in imbalanced log datasets. The authors investigate rare log groups (fewer than 5 instances) which constitute nearly 20% of templates but less than 0.01% of messages. They propose TAILOR, a framework using template-preserving augmentation to improve template inference for rare log groups, achieving 19% accuracy improvement while maintaining competitive overall performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world log datasets exhibit highly imbalanced, long-tailed distributions where frequent templates dominate benchmark metrics while poor performance on rare yet operationally important events remains hidden. This imbalance causes evaluation results to be overly optimistic and critical rare events may be missed.

Method: The authors first conducted an empirical study on Loghub-2.0 benchmark to quantify the prevalence and impact of rare log groups. They then proposed TAILOR, a log parsing framework that improves template inference for rare log groups through template-preserving augmentation - enriching rare log groups with template-consistent log messages before template inference to provide additional structural evidence for distinguishing static tokens from dynamic variables.

Result: Rare log groups account for nearly 20% of all templates but less than 0.01% of log messages. TAILOR improves parsing accuracy on rare log groups by 19% over the strongest baseline while maintaining competitive performance on complete datasets. The augmentation strategy generalizes across different LLM backbones and consistently improves existing LLM-based parsers without modifying their core architectures.

Conclusion: The proposed TAILOR framework effectively addresses the rare log group parsing problem through template-preserving augmentation. By enriching rare log groups with additional structural evidence, the method significantly improves parsing accuracy for rare templates while maintaining overall performance. The approach is generalizable and can be applied to existing LLM-based parsers without architectural changes.

Abstract: Log parsing is essential for system log analysis because it supports tasks such as debugging, monitoring, and anomaly detection by transforming unstructured log messages into structured log templates. However, real-world log datasets exhibit highly imbalanced, long-tailed distributions, where a small number of frequent templates dominate while many rare templates appear only a few times. This imbalance causes evaluation results to be overly optimistic by allowing frequent templates to dominate benchmark metrics, while poor performance on rare yet operationally important events remains largely hidden. In this paper, we investigate the prevalence and impact of rare log groups, defined as log groups with fewer than five instances. Our empirical study on the widely used Loghub-2.0 benchmark shows that rare log groups account for nearly 20% of all templates but less than 0.01% of log messages. Because they contain only a handful of instances, all evaluated parsers experience substantial performance degradation on these groups. To address this challenge, we propose TAILOR, a log parsing framework that improves template inference for rare log groups through template-preserving augmentation. TAILOR enriches rare log groups with template- consistent log messages before template inference. The additional structural evidence helps distinguish static tokens from dynamic variables. Our experimental results show that TAILOR improves parsing accuracy on rare log groups by 19% over the strongest baseline while maintaining competitive performance on complete datasets. We further show that the proposed augmentation strategy generalizes across different LLM backbones and consistently improves existing LLM-based parsers without modifying their core architectures.

</details>


### [71] [The Vocabulary of Flaky Tests in Swift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25516)
*João Medeiros,Denini Silva,Breno Miranda*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: This paper addresses flaky tests in Swift by applying vocabulary-based machine learning prediction. The study collects 91 flaky and 22,349 stable tests from 15 open-source Swift projects, trains five classifiers on TF-IDF features, and achieves best performance with Random Forest (Precision=0.92, F1=0.86, AUC=0.95). Key flakiness markers include async/await, expectation-based synchronization, and timing constructs, while stability markers include synchronous assertion vocabulary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
